---------- turn 15 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F43.1, F43.2, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I came in because I don’t feel like myself anymore. Ever since they changed my hours over at the warehouse and put in this new inventory system, it’s like everything’s been coming apart on me. I’ve been a stocker most of my life, you know, and I’m used to hard work, I’m used to changes here and there, but this has just… it’s got me all turned around.\n\nI’m missing shifts, which is not like me, and I’ve missed commitments at church too, things I normally wouldn’t miss. My family’s noticed I’ve been snapping at them, and I hate that, because they don’t deserve it. Some mornings I get dressed for work, boots on and everything, and then I just sit there, kind of frozen, wondering if I can even make myself go into that place again. It feels like I’m falling apart over something I ought to be able to handle, but I’m not handling it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge, especially when thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. Especially when I start thinking about having to go back in there. It’s not like I’m afraid of work itself—I’ve worked in warehouses most of my life, and I’ve always been the kind of person who shows up and does what needs doing. But ever since they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, I just feel all turned around.\n\nSome mornings I’ll get dressed like I’m going—boots on, lunch maybe sitting there—and then I just sit down and can’t seem to move. I start thinking about walking into that place, dealing with the schedule, the system, all of it, and it feels like I’m falling apart. I’ve missed shifts, and that’s not who I am. I’ve even missed things at church I said I’d be there for, and I keep snapping at my family over nothing. So yes, when work comes into my mind, I get on edge, and then I feel ashamed because I keep thinking I ought to be able to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding places or situations that remind you of work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do. I mean, it’s mostly the warehouse itself, or anything that gets me thinking about going in there. Ever since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, it’s like the whole place feels different to me, even though I’ve worked that kind of job most of my life.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work already, boots on and everything, and I’ll just sit there. I’m not even doing anything, just sitting, thinking about walking into that place and dealing with all of it again, and I can’t seem to make myself move. Then I miss a shift, and I feel terrible about it, because that’s not how I’ve been. I’ve always tried to show up.\n\nAnd it’s not just work, exactly. It’s like once I start avoiding that, other things slip too. I’ve missed church commitments I normally would’ve kept, and then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them when they don’t deserve it. So yes, I guess I am avoiding things tied to work, because facing it feels like too much sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, doctor. Detached from myself or the world… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m sitting there and everything feels kind of heavy or hard to get started, but I don’t think I’ve felt like I was outside my body or like the world wasn’t real, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I do sort of zone out, I guess, especially when I’m overwhelmed, but it feels more like my mind is tired or I’m stuck in my own thoughts. Not like I’m watching myself from across the room or anything like that. So… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of detached feeling, at least not in a way I could clearly describe.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think so. I’ve never had that feeling like I’m floating above myself or watching myself from across the room or anything like that.\n\nI mean, sometimes I can get kind of lost in my head, and I might sit there for a while before I realize how much time has passed, but it doesn’t feel like I’ve left my body. It’s more like I’m just stuck thinking, if that makes sense. But observing myself from outside—no, I can’t say I’ve experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your mood, such as feelings of sadness or hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yes… my mood has definitely changed. I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless all the time, but I’ve been feeling pretty low, pretty worn down. Ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and brought in that new inventory system, I feel like I’m just not myself anymore.\n\nI came in because it feels like I’m falling apart over something I keep telling myself I should be able to handle. I’m missing shifts, and that bothers me a lot because I’ve always been the kind of man who shows up and does the work. And then I’ve missed church commitments too, which isn’t like me either. Some mornings I’m sitting there with my work boots on, ready to go, and I just sit there wondering if I can even face walking into that place again.\n\nAnd then when I’m home, I’m not easy to be around. I snap at my family, and afterward I feel bad about it because they didn’t cause any of this. So yes, I’d say there’s sadness there, and a kind of discouragement. It’s like I’m watching parts of my life slip out of my hands, and I don’t seem to know how to get a grip on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and made a list of things I used to enjoy versus now. I guess there are things I don’t get to as much, but I don’t know if that’s because I’ve lost interest exactly, or because everything just feels harder to organize around lately.\n\nLike, if you’re asking whether I suddenly don’t care about anything anymore, I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I still care about the things I’ve always cared about. But I may not be showing up to them the same way, if that makes sense. Sometimes I’m just not in the right frame of mind, or I put things off, and then afterward I think, “Well, why didn’t I just go do that?” \n\nSo… maybe some, but I’m not sure I’d call it losing interest. It’s more like I haven’t been keeping up with things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track or weighing myself every morning or anything like that. At my age, weight can move around a little, and I don’t always pay attention unless my pants suddenly fit different or someone says something.\n\nI don’t think there’s been any big change, though. Nothing where I looked in the mirror and thought, “Oh, I’ve really lost weight,” or the opposite. My clothes seem about the same, as far as I can tell. So… no significant change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I’m 74, so “tired” is a little hard to judge sometimes. There are days where I think, well, maybe this is just being older, or maybe I didn’t sleep as well, or maybe the day took more out of me than I expected.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic thing where I suddenly can’t keep my eyes open all day or anything like that. It’s more that I feel worn down in general, but whether that’s actual tiredness in the body or just everything feeling like a lot lately, I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it the way maybe I should.\n\nSo… more tired than usual? Maybe a little, but I couldn’t say for certain. I haven’t noticed a specific big change that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things you've done or haven't done lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, “I did this terrible thing,” or anything like that.\n\nI do second-guess myself, though. I mean, I’ll think about things I maybe should’ve handled better, or things I didn’t get done, and I can turn that over in my head more than I probably need to. But I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like… frustration with myself, maybe. Or wondering if I’m doing enough, or if I’m letting things slide.\n\nAt 74, you can look back on a lot of things and find something to criticize if you want to. But lately, I can’t point to one specific thing I feel guilty about. It’s not like there’s some big wrongdoing weighing on me. So I’d say… not especially, no. Maybe some regret or self-questioning here and there, but not guilt in a strong sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events recently or in the past that might be connected to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call anything traumatic, no. I mean, I’ve had difficult things happen in life, like anybody who’s been around 74 years has. You don’t get to this age without some losses or hard periods or things you’d rather not go through again. But if you mean something like a terrible accident, violence, being in danger, something like that… nothing recent comes to mind.\n\nAnd I can’t really point to one past event and say, “That’s what this is connected to.” I’ve wondered about that myself a little, because when you feel off, you start looking for reasons. But I don’t have some clear memory or incident that keeps coming back or anything like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not that I know of. Nothing specific that I would identify as a trauma connected to how I’ve been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific aspects of the warehouse or your job that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there are. It’s hard to separate the place from what changed, if that makes sense. I used to know how to do my job. I mean, I’m a stocker, order filler, I’ve been around warehouses and routines long enough that I could keep myself steady. But when they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, it just threw me completely off.\n\nSo it isn’t like I’m avoiding one aisle or one person exactly. It’s more the whole setup now—the new schedule, trying to keep up with that system, feeling like I’m already behind before I even clock in. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. Maybe more than I wanted to admit at first.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Disconnected from my thoughts or feelings… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d put it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, there are times when I’m so caught up in everything that I don’t know what I’m feeling right away. I’ll sit there and try to sort it out, and it’s like there’s too much going on in my head, if that makes sense. But I don’t feel like I’m outside myself or like my thoughts aren’t mine. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. It’s more the way the job has become since the schedule and system changed. That’s what I’m having trouble facing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

{"candidates": ["F43.0", "F43.1", "F43.2", "F43.8"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

But when they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, it just threw me completely off.\n\nSo it isn’t like I’m avoiding one aisle or one person exactly. It’s more the whole setup now—the new schedule, trying to keep up with that system, feeling like I’m already behind before I even clock in. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. Maybe more than I wanted to admit at first.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Disconnected from my thoughts or feelings… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d put it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, there are times when I’m so caught up in everything that I don’t know what I’m feeling right away. I’ll sit there and try to sort it out, and it’s like there’s too much going on in my head, if that makes sense. But I don’t feel like I’m outside myself or like my thoughts aren’t mine. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everyday things in general more than I should. I mean… work, yes, that’s on my mind a lot, but that’s also because work has become such a problem lately. So I don’t know if that counts as everyday worrying or just… worrying because there’s actually something going wrong.

With family, I do think about whether I’m letting people down or whether they’re noticing I’m not myself, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around worrying about every little family matter or imagining disasters all day. It’s more tied to the situation I’m already dealing with.

So… I guess I’d say I worry, yes, but I’m not sure it’s the kind of constant worry about everything that you might mean. It feels more focused on the things that have been difficult lately.

I mean… I haven’t sat down and made a list of things I used to enjoy versus now. I mean, I’m 74, so “tired” is a little hard to judge sometimes. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. It’s more the way the job has become since the schedule and system changed. That’s what I’m having trouble facing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like work or family, more than you think you should?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everyday things in general more than I should. I mean… work, yes, that’s on my mind a lot, but that’s also because work has become such a problem lately. So I don’t know if that counts as everyday worrying or just… worrying because there’s actually something going wrong.\n\nWith family, I do think about whether I’m letting people down or whether they’re noticing I’m not myself, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around worrying about every little family matter or imagining disasters all day. It’s more tied to the situation I’m already dealing with.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I worry, yes, but I’m not sure it’s the kind of constant worry about everything that you might mean. It feels more focused on the things that have been difficult lately.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you normally enjoy?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 74 / stocker_or_order_filler / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know that I’ve noticed that exactly. I mean, I still like the things I like, if that makes sense. It’s not like I suddenly don’t care about anything or I’ve decided I don’t enjoy my usual stuff anymore.

Sometimes I don’t get around to things, or I’ll sit there longer than I mean to before doing something, but I’m not sure that’s the same as losing interest. If someone asked me, “Do you still enjoy that?” I’d probably say yes. I just maybe haven’t been paying close attention to it because my mind’s been so occupied with everything else.

So… no, I don’t think I’ve had a clear decrease in interest. At least not one I could point to and say, “That’s definitely changed.”

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I came in because I don’t feel like myself anymore. Ever since they changed my hours over at the warehouse and put in this new inventory system, it’s like everything’s been coming apart on me. I’ve been a stocker most of my life, you know, and I’m used to hard work, I’m used to changes here and there, but this has just… it’s got me all turned around.\n\nI’m missing shifts, which is not like me, and I’ve missed commitments at church too, things I normally wouldn’t miss. My family’s noticed I’ve been snapping at them, and I hate that, because they don’t deserve it. Some mornings I get dressed for work, boots on and everything, and then I just sit there, kind of frozen, wondering if I can even make myself go into that place again. It feels like I’m falling apart over something I ought to be able to handle, but I’m not handling it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge, especially when thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. Especially when I start thinking about having to go back in there. It’s not like I’m afraid of work itself—I’ve worked in warehouses most of my life, and I’ve always been the kind of person who shows up and does what needs doing. I mean, it’s mostly the warehouse itself, or anything that gets me thinking about going in there. Ever since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, it’s like the whole place feels different to me, even though I’ve worked that kind of job most of my life.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work already, boots on and everything, and I’ll just sit there. I’m not even doing anything, just sitting, thinking about walking into that place and dealing with all of it again, and I can’t seem to make myself move. Then I miss a shift, and I feel terrible about it, because that’s not how I’ve been. I’ve always tried to show up.\n\nAnd it’s not just work, exactly. It’s like once I start avoiding that, other things slip too. I’ve missed church commitments I normally would’ve kept, and then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them when they don’t deserve it. So yes, I guess I am avoiding things tied to work, because facing it feels like too much sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or the world around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, doctor. Detached from myself or the world… I don’t know. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m sitting there and everything feels kind of heavy or hard to get started, but I don’t think I’ve felt like I was outside my body or like the world wasn’t real, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I do sort of zone out, I guess, especially when I’m overwhelmed, but it feels more like my mind is tired or I’m stuck in my own thoughts. Not like I’m watching myself from across the room or anything like that. So… no, I haven’t really noticed that kind of detached feeling, at least not in a way I could clearly describe.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think so. I’ve never had that feeling like I’m floating above myself or watching myself from across the room or anything like that.\n\nI mean, sometimes I can get kind of lost in my head, and I might sit there for a while before I realize how much time has passed, but it doesn’t feel like I’ve left my body. It’s more like I’m just stuck thinking, if that makes sense. But observing myself from outside—no, I can’t say I’ve experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your mood, such as feelings of sadness or hopelessness?\n\nPatient: Yes… my mood has definitely changed. I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless all the time, but I’ve been feeling pretty low, pretty worn down. Ever since they changed my hours at the warehouse and brought in that new inventory system, I feel like I’m just not myself anymore.\n\nI came in because it feels like I’m falling apart over something I keep telling myself I should be able to handle. I’m missing shifts, and that bothers me a lot because I’ve always been the kind of man who shows up and does the work. And then I’ve missed church commitments too, which isn’t like me either. Some mornings I’m sitting there with my work boots on, ready to go, and I just sit there wondering if I can even face walking into that place again.\n\nAnd then when I’m home, I’m not easy to be around. I snap at my family, and afterward I feel bad about it because they didn’t cause any of this. So yes, I’d say there’s sadness there, and a kind of discouragement. It’s like I’m watching parts of my life slip out of my hands, and I don’t seem to know how to get a grip on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and made a list of things I used to enjoy versus now. I guess there are things I don’t get to as much, but I don’t know if that’s because I’ve lost interest exactly, or because everything just feels harder to organize around lately.\n\nLike, if you’re asking whether I suddenly don’t care about anything anymore, I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I still care about the things I’ve always cared about. But I may not be showing up to them the same way, if that makes sense. Sometimes I’m just not in the right frame of mind, or I put things off, and then afterward I think, “Well, why didn’t I just go do that?” \n\nSo… maybe some, but I’m not sure I’d call it losing interest. It’s more like I haven’t been keeping up with things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track or weighing myself every morning or anything like that. At my age, weight can move around a little, and I don’t always pay attention unless my pants suddenly fit different or someone says something.\n\nI don’t think there’s been any big change, though. Nothing where I looked in the mirror and thought, “Oh, I’ve really lost weight,” or the opposite. My clothes seem about the same, as far as I can tell. So… no significant change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I’m 74, so “tired” is a little hard to judge sometimes. There are days where I think, well, maybe this is just being older, or maybe I didn’t sleep as well, or maybe the day took more out of me than I expected.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic thing where I suddenly can’t keep my eyes open all day or anything like that. It’s more that I feel worn down in general, but whether that’s actual tiredness in the body or just everything feeling like a lot lately, I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it the way maybe I should.\n\nSo… more tired than usual? Maybe a little, but I couldn’t say for certain. I haven’t noticed a specific big change that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things you've done or haven't done lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, “I did this terrible thing,” or anything like that.\n\nI do second-guess myself, though. I mean, I’ll think about things I maybe should’ve handled better, or things I didn’t get done, and I can turn that over in my head more than I probably need to. But I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like… frustration with myself, maybe. Or wondering if I’m doing enough, or if I’m letting things slide.\n\nAt 74, you can look back on a lot of things and find something to criticize if you want to. But lately, I can’t point to one specific thing I feel guilty about. It’s not like there’s some big wrongdoing weighing on me. So I’d say… not especially, no. Maybe some regret or self-questioning here and there, but not guilt in a strong sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events recently or in the past that might be connected to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call anything traumatic, no. I mean, I’ve had difficult things happen in life, like anybody who’s been around 74 years has. You don’t get to this age without some losses or hard periods or things you’d rather not go through again. But if you mean something like a terrible accident, violence, being in danger, something like that… nothing recent comes to mind.\n\nAnd I can’t really point to one past event and say, “That’s what this is connected to.” I’ve wondered about that myself a little, because when you feel off, you start looking for reasons. But I don’t have some clear memory or incident that keeps coming back or anything like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not that I know of. Nothing specific that I would identify as a trauma connected to how I’ve been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific aspects of the warehouse or your job that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there are. It’s hard to separate the place from what changed, if that makes sense. I used to know how to do my job. I mean, I’m a stocker, order filler, I’ve been around warehouses and routines long enough that I could keep myself steady. But when they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, it just threw me completely off.\n\nSo it isn’t like I’m avoiding one aisle or one person exactly. It’s more the whole setup now—the new schedule, trying to keep up with that system, feeling like I’m already behind before I even clock in. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. Maybe more than I wanted to admit at first.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Disconnected from my thoughts or feelings… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d put it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, there are times when I’m so caught up in everything that I don’t know what I’m feeling right away. I’ll sit there and try to sort it out, and it’s like there’s too much going on in my head, if that makes sense. But I don’t feel like I’m outside myself or like my thoughts aren’t mine. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. At my age, weight can move around a little, and I don’t always pay attention unless my pants suddenly fit different or someone says something.\n\nI don’t think there’s been any big change, though. Nothing where I looked in the mirror and thought, “Oh, I’ve really lost weight,” or the opposite. My clothes seem about the same, as far as I can tell. So… no significant change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I’m 74, so “tired” is a little hard to judge sometimes. There are days where I think, well, maybe this is just being older, or maybe I didn’t sleep as well, or maybe the day took more out of me than I expected.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic thing where I suddenly can’t keep my eyes open all day or anything like that. It’s more that I feel worn down in general, but whether that’s actual tiredness in the body or just everything feeling like a lot lately, I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it the way maybe I should.\n\nSo… more tired than usual? Maybe a little, but I couldn’t say for certain. I haven’t noticed a specific big change that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things you've done or haven't done lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, “I did this terrible thing,” or anything like that.\n\nI do second-guess myself, though. I mean, I’ll think about things I maybe should’ve handled better, or things I didn’t get done, and I can turn that over in my head more than I probably need to. But I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like… frustration with myself, maybe. Or wondering if I’m doing enough, or if I’m letting things slide.\n\nAt 74, you can look back on a lot of things and find something to criticize if you want to. But lately, I can’t point to one specific thing I feel guilty about. It’s not like there’s some big wrongdoing weighing on me. So I’d say… not especially, no. Maybe some regret or self-questioning here and there, but not guilt in a strong sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events recently or in the past that might be connected to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call anything traumatic, no. I mean, I’ve had difficult things happen in life, like anybody who’s been around 74 years has. You don’t get to this age without some losses or hard periods or things you’d rather not go through again. But if you mean something like a terrible accident, violence, being in danger, something like that… nothing recent comes to mind.\n\nAnd I can’t really point to one past event and say, “That’s what this is connected to.” I’ve wondered about that myself a little, because when you feel off, you start looking for reasons. But I don’t have some clear memory or incident that keeps coming back or anything like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not that I know of. Nothing specific that I would identify as a trauma connected to how I’ve been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific aspects of the warehouse or your job that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there are. It’s hard to separate the place from what changed, if that makes sense. I used to know how to do my job. I mean, I’m a stocker, order filler, I’ve been around warehouses and routines long enough that I could keep myself steady. But when they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, it just threw me completely off.\n\nSo it isn’t like I’m avoiding one aisle or one person exactly. It’s more the whole setup now—the new schedule, trying to keep up with that system, feeling like I’m already behind before I even clock in. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. Maybe more than I wanted to admit at first.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Disconnected from my thoughts or feelings… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d put it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, there are times when I’m so caught up in everything that I don’t know what I’m feeling right away. I’ll sit there and try to sort it out, and it’s like there’s too much going on in my head, if that makes sense. But I don’t feel like I’m outside myself or like my thoughts aren’t mine. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. It’s more the way the job has become since the schedule and system changed. That’s what I’m having trouble facing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like work or family, more than you think you should?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everyday things in general more than I should. I mean… work, yes, that’s on my mind a lot, but that’s also because work has become such a problem lately. So I don’t know if that counts as everyday worrying or just… worrying because there’s actually something going wrong.\n\nWith family, I do think about whether I’m letting people down or whether they’re noticing I’m not myself, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting around worrying about every little family matter or imagining disasters all day. It’s more tied to the situation I’m already dealing with.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I worry, yes, but I’m not sure it’s the kind of constant worry about everything that you might mean. It feels more focused on the things that have been difficult lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. That’s really what started all of this, I think. It was when they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in that new inventory system. Before that, I’m not saying everything was perfect, but I knew my routine. I knew when I was going in, I knew how the work flowed, I knew what I was doing.

Then the schedule changed, and the system changed, and it felt like the whole floor changed with it. Suddenly I was having to keep up with things in a way that just didn’t feel familiar anymore. And I know people probably think, “Well, everybody has to adjust,” and I get that, I really do. But for me it’s like something got knocked loose. I started feeling like I was falling apart.

I came in because since all that happened, I’ve been missing shifts, and that’s not like me. I’ve missed church commitments too, which bothers me a lot. I’ve been short with my family, snapping at them over things I shouldn’t. And some mornings I’ll be sitting there already dressed for work, boots on, just staring and wondering if I can even make myself walk into that place.

So yes, it was those work changes—the hours and the new inventory system. That’s what seemed to set this off.

It’s like I’m watching parts of my life slip out of my hands, and I don’t seem to know how to get a grip on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean… I haven’t sat down and made a list of things I used to enjoy versus now. I guess there are things I don’t get to as much, but I don’t know if that’s because I’ve lost interest exactly, or because everything just feels harder to organize around lately.\n\nLike, if you’re asking whether I suddenly don’t care about anything anymore, I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I still care about the things I’ve always cared about. But I may not be showing up to them the same way, if that makes sense. Sometimes I’m just not in the right frame of mind, or I put things off, and then afterward I think, “Well, why didn’t I just go do that?” \n\nSo… maybe some, but I’m not sure I’d call it losing interest. It’s more like I haven’t been keeping up with things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, I haven’t been keeping close track or weighing myself every morning or anything like that. At my age, weight can move around a little, and I don’t always pay attention unless my pants suddenly fit different or someone says something.\n\nI don’t think there’s been any big change, though. Nothing where I looked in the mirror and thought, “Oh, I’ve really lost weight,” or the opposite. My clothes seem about the same, as far as I can tell. So… no significant change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I’m 74, so “tired” is a little hard to judge sometimes. There are days where I think, well, maybe this is just being older, or maybe I didn’t sleep as well, or maybe the day took more out of me than I expected.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear, dramatic thing where I suddenly can’t keep my eyes open all day or anything like that. It’s more that I feel worn down in general, but whether that’s actual tiredness in the body or just everything feeling like a lot lately, I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it the way maybe I should.\n\nSo… more tired than usual? Maybe a little, but I couldn’t say for certain. I haven’t noticed a specific big change that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things you've done or haven't done lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I don’t know. Not in a clear way, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, “I did this terrible thing,” or anything like that.\n\nI do second-guess myself, though. I mean, I’ll think about things I maybe should’ve handled better, or things I didn’t get done, and I can turn that over in my head more than I probably need to. But I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like… frustration with myself, maybe. Or wondering if I’m doing enough, or if I’m letting things slide.\n\nAt 74, you can look back on a lot of things and find something to criticize if you want to. But lately, I can’t point to one specific thing I feel guilty about. It’s not like there’s some big wrongdoing weighing on me. So I’d say… not especially, no. Maybe some regret or self-questioning here and there, but not guilt in a strong sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events recently or in the past that might be connected to your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call anything traumatic, no. I mean, I’ve had difficult things happen in life, like anybody who’s been around 74 years has. You don’t get to this age without some losses or hard periods or things you’d rather not go through again. But if you mean something like a terrible accident, violence, being in danger, something like that… nothing recent comes to mind.\n\nAnd I can’t really point to one past event and say, “That’s what this is connected to.” I’ve wondered about that myself a little, because when you feel off, you start looking for reasons. But I don’t have some clear memory or incident that keeps coming back or anything like that.\n\nSo I guess my answer is… not that I know of. Nothing specific that I would identify as a trauma connected to how I’ve been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific aspects of the warehouse or your job that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think there are. It’s hard to separate the place from what changed, if that makes sense. I used to know how to do my job. I mean, I’m a stocker, order filler, I’ve been around warehouses and routines long enough that I could keep myself steady. But when they changed my hours and put in that new inventory system, it just threw me completely off.\n\nSo it isn’t like I’m avoiding one aisle or one person exactly. It’s more the whole setup now—the new schedule, trying to keep up with that system, feeling like I’m already behind before I even clock in. Some mornings I get my work boots on and then I just sit there, looking at the floor, thinking, “Can I really face that place today?” And then I’m late, or I miss a shift, and that makes everything worse.\n\nI’ve also been missing things at church, commitments I normally wouldn’t miss, because I’m either tied up with the warehouse schedule or I’m just so worked up about it I don’t follow through. And then I come home and I’m short with my family, snapping at them over things that aren’t their fault.\n\nSo yes, I guess I am avoiding it—not in a planned way, not like I decided to stop caring. It’s more like I feel like I’m falling apart around the job now, and facing that building and that new system just feels bigger than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your current struggles are significantly impacting your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I’d say it is. I don’t really like admitting that, because I’ve always been someone people could count on, at work and at church especially. But lately it’s gotten to where it’s not just me feeling bad inside—it’s showing up in real ways.\n\nMy supervisor has actually documented issues with my reliability, which was hard to hear. Being late, missing shifts, not being as dependable as I used to be… and he’s talked to me about maybe taking medical leave. That hit me pretty hard, because I never saw myself as the kind of person who’d be in that position. I’ve worked a long time, and I’ve always tried to be steady.\n\nAnd then with church, I’ve pulled back from duties people were counting on me for. That bothers me a lot. Those weren’t just little optional things to me—they were commitments. People expected me to be there and help, and I haven’t been following through the way I normally would.\n\nSo yes, it’s definitely affecting my daily life and relationships. Maybe more than I wanted to admit at first.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Disconnected from my thoughts or feelings… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d put it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, there are times when I’m so caught up in everything that I don’t know what I’m feeling right away. I’ll sit there and try to sort it out, and it’s like there’s too much going on in my head, if that makes sense. But I don’t feel like I’m outside myself or like my thoughts aren’t mine. It’s more that I get overwhelmed and have trouble putting words to it in the moment.\n\nSometimes my family will ask me what’s wrong, and I’ll say, “I don’t know,” not because there’s nothing wrong, but because I honestly can’t organize it quickly enough to explain. But disconnected… I don’t know. Maybe I’d need you to explain what you mean by that a little more. I haven’t noticed anything like feeling unreal or separated from myself, if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding specific tasks or areas within the warehouse itself?\n\nPatient: Not one specific task, exactly… I mean, I wish I could point to one thing and say, “It’s that aisle,” or “It’s only the scanning,” but it feels bigger than that.\n\nEver since they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system, the whole job feels different to me. Things I used to do without thinking now feel like I’m already behind before I even start. So I don’t know if I’m avoiding one area of the warehouse as much as I’m avoiding walking into that whole situation—clocking in, dealing with the new system, trying to keep up, feeling like I’m going to mess something up.\n\nSome mornings I’ll be dressed for work, boots on and everything, and I just sit there thinking, “Can I even face that place today?” And then I end up late, or missing the shift, and then that makes me feel worse.\n\nSo… no, not a particular shelf or corner of the warehouse. It’s more the way the job has become since the schedule and system changed. That’s what I’m having trouble facing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like work or family, more than you think you should?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everyday things in general more than I should. It feels more focused on the things that have been difficult lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that exactly. It’s not like I suddenly don’t care about anything or I’ve decided I don’t enjoy my usual stuff anymore.\n\nSometimes I don’t get around to things, or I’ll sit there longer than I mean to before doing something, but I’m not sure that’s the same as losing interest. I just maybe haven’t been paying close attention to it because my mind’s been so occupied with everything else.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a clear decrease in interest. I’ve missed church commitments too, which bothers me a lot. I’ve been short with my family, snapping at them over things I shouldn’t. I honestly don’t know, because I haven’t really had a chance to find out. The new schedule is still in place, and the inventory system is still what we’re using, so it’s not like I’ve had a week or two where things went back to the way they were and I could see, “Oh, okay, now I feel more like myself again.”

It feels more focused on the things that have been difficult lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or hobbies that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that exactly. I mean, I still like the things I like, if that makes sense. It’s not like I suddenly don’t care about anything or I’ve decided I don’t enjoy my usual stuff anymore.\n\nSometimes I don’t get around to things, or I’ll sit there longer than I mean to before doing something, but I’m not sure that’s the same as losing interest. If someone asked me, “Do you still enjoy that?” I’d probably say yes. I just maybe haven’t been paying close attention to it because my mind’s been so occupied with everything else.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had a clear decrease in interest. At least not one I could point to and say, “That’s definitely changed.”\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific event or change at work that triggered your current feelings?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s really what started all of this, I think. It was when they changed my hours at the warehouse and put in that new inventory system. Before that, I’m not saying everything was perfect, but I knew my routine. I knew when I was going in, I knew how the work flowed, I knew what I was doing.\n\nThen the schedule changed, and the system changed, and it felt like the whole floor changed with it. Suddenly I was having to keep up with things in a way that just didn’t feel familiar anymore. And I know people probably think, “Well, everybody has to adjust,” and I get that, I really do. But for me it’s like something got knocked loose. I started feeling like I was falling apart.\n\nI came in because since all that happened, I’ve been missing shifts, and that’s not like me. I’ve missed church commitments too, which bothers me a lot. I’ve been short with my family, snapping at them over things I shouldn’t. And some mornings I’ll be sitting there already dressed for work, boots on, just staring and wondering if I can even make myself walk into that place.\n\nSo yes, it was those work changes—the hours and the new inventory system. That’s what seemed to set this off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties would improve if the warehouse reverted to its old schedule and inventory system?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know, because I haven’t really had a chance to find out. The new schedule is still in place, and the inventory system is still what we’re using, so it’s not like I’ve had a week or two where things went back to the way they were and I could see, “Oh, okay, now I feel more like myself again.”\n\nPart of me thinks, yes, maybe if it all went back to the old way, I’d do better, because at least I understood that routine. But I don’t want to say that like I know for sure, because I haven’t had a real break from it. Even when I’m not physically at the warehouse, I’m still dealing with the effects of it, if that makes sense. The schedule is still hanging over me, and the fallout from the changes hasn’t really settled down.\n\nSo… maybe it would help. I think it probably would help some. But I can’t say for certain, because I’m still right in the middle of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yes. It was pretty soon after, definitely within that kind of time frame. I don’t remember it like a calendar date circled in red or anything, but I know I started coming apart within the first few weeks after they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system.
